# Scheduler stability investigation — 2026-09-13

Read-only investigation of every recurring scheduler instability class (nine class readers + one poller reader), a synthesis into structural PRDs, and two adversarial verifications per PRD (premise: does the problem still exist in HEAD; design: would the mechanism break an invariant). Companion to the 33-PRD file-organization chain; the evidence base the "ss-*" scheduler-stability PRDs cite.

Headline numbers: 109 fix commits touched the scheduler modules in the 30 days to 2026-09-13; over 288 runs since August the executor exited 0 with a landed commit in 28 runs that were still parked non-clean by transcript heuristics; 173 investigation probes were spawned; total executor spend ≈ $1,940, of which fix-plan runs ≈ $94. history.jsonl recorded 183 completed / 7 failed / 0 needs_review (needs_review was never archived until PRD 1203).

This file is a frozen findings record (see reviews/README.md). Line numbers were current on 2026-09-13 and drift; grep the quoted function/constant names.

## Investigation reports by class

### dispatch liveness

**Root-cause category:** Edge-triggered dispatch with no owning level-triggered reconcile loop: every tick is a side-effect of some other subsystem's event (billing poll, reaper, child exit, slot release, pause timer), so dispatch liveness is the transitive closure of five unrelated subsystems' health, and the one fixed-cadence backstop (the 60 s heartbeat) is threshold-gated on a timestamp that every no-op tick refreshes.

**Current mechanism:** HEAD has NO unconditional, fixed-cadence, level-triggered dispatch tick. `tickQueue` (src/main/scheduler.cjs, serialized behind module-level `tickTail`) has exactly 7 call sites, all edge-triggered: 1. `spawnJob`'s `finally` (scheduler.cjs:7169) — a job finished. 2. `reapDeadRunningJobs`'s trailing `tickQueue().catch(()=>{})` (scheduler.cjs:8313) — LAST statement inside that function's `try`. This is the closest thing to a level-triggered driver: `pollLoop` (scheduler.cjs:8333) `await reapDeadRunningJobs()` is the FIRST statement of every poll cycle, before any billing/policy branching, so in the happy path a tick happens once per poll cycle regardless of gates. It is a side-effect of the reaper, not a named driver, and it is skipped entirely if anything in reapDeadRunningJobs' ~380-line body (readQueue, selectReapableJobs, salvage git calls, mutate) throws first — the `catch (e)` at the bottom swallows and returns without ticking. 3. `maybeLaunchWhenAvailable` (scheduler.cjs:7480→7491) — called from 5 branches of `pollLoop`; gated on `firePolicy==='when-available'`, `!state.paused`, `pending.length>0`, `cachedUtilization != null`, `cachedUtilization < utilizationThreshold`. 4. `runDueJobs` (scheduler.cjs:7460→7471) — human force-tick/run-now IPC, `resumeTimer` (scheduler.cjs:3244), `fireTimer` (scheduler.cjs:3126, only under legacy `firePolicy:'on-reset'`), and `powerMonitor.on('resume')`. 5. `runQueueStarvationWatchdog` (scheduler.cjs:7728→7786) — the ONLY caller on a truly fixed cadence: `heartbeatInterval = setInterval(..., 60_000)` (scheduler.cjs:10540) calls it every 60 s via `readQueueSync()`. But it only ticks when `classifyQueueStarvationByProject` (scheduler.cjs:7573) returns a `kind:'starved'` verdict, which requires per-project `runningCount===0`, `pending>0`, `dispatchable>0`, `!paused`, AND machine-wide `idleMs = now - Date.parse(state.lastDispatchAttemptAt) >= QUEUE_STARVATION_MS` (10 min, scheduler.cjs:7499). Before ticking it force-clears `cancelToken.cancelled = false` (scheduler.cjs:7782) and coerces `cachedUtilization` from null→0. 6. `sessionSlots.subscribe(() => tickQueue())` (scheduler.cjs:10125) — fires on `sessionSlots.release()` / `setCap()` (src/main/lib/sessionSlots.cjs `release`/`setCap`/`listeners`). 7. Investigation-child exit (scheduler.cjs:5630). `tickQueue` itself stamps `lastDispatchAttemptAt` (scheduler.cjs:7290) immediately after the unreadable/paused/cancelled guards and BEFORE every remaining gate, then stamps `lastRu

**Landed fixes:**
- [structural] Gave the starvation watchdog a driver independent of the billing poller: moved runQueueStarvationWatchdog onto the 60 s heartbeatInterval (scheduler.cjs:10540→10548), switched its idle clock from lastRunAt to lastDispatchAttemptAt, and made it force-clear a stuck cancelToken (scheduler.cjs:7782) bef (f18e161 (2026-09-11) fix(scheduler): give dispatch a driver independent of the billing poller, fix the watchdog's own bl)
- [structural] Made lastDispatchAttemptAt durable — queueStore.readMerged/readMergedSync/writeSplit were dropping it, so the watchdog's idle clock reset to Infinity/NaN across every read-write cycle. (ecb1c10 (2026-09-12) fix(queueStore): stop dropping lastDispatchAttemptAt on read and write)
- [structural] Partitioned starvation DETECTION per-cwd (classifyQueueStarvationByProject, scheduler.cjs:7573) so one long job in any project no longer disarms the watchdog for every other project — the observed 2026-09-12 starry-night-ships incident. (acefa41 (2026-09-12) test(scheduler): cover per-project queue-starvation detection)
- [structural] Worktree cap now self-heals: reserveWorktreeSlot (src/main/lib/gitWorktree.cjs) re-derives activeWorktreeCount from getObservedWorktreeCount() whenever the in-memory counter is at/over cap, under withKindLock. A leaked counter no longer means machine-wide dispatch death until restart. (2cc28e1 (2026-09-11) fix(scheduler): make the worktree cap self-heal against observed disk state)
- [structural] scheduler-machine.json torn-write fixed at the source, plus findLongestValidJsonPrefix/buildMachineStateRecovery/recoverTornMachineStateSync in queueStore.cjs: an unrecoverable machine file now falls back to defaults rather than marking state.unreadable, explicitly 'so dispatch does not silently hal (f56bdc0 (2026-09-11) fix(scheduler): stop scheduler-machine.json torn-file corruption at the source)
- [patch] Bounded per-project starve escalation (selectStarveEscalations / runStarveEscalationSweep / starveEscalated latch, STARVE_ESCALATION_MS) — audits project_starve_escalated and toasts schedule:stall with lastTick.reason. Notification only; dispatches nothing. (a143552 (2026-09-12) feat(scheduler): bounded escalation for starved-project starvation)
- [patch] classifyQueueHealth (scheduler.cjs:7653) names WHY the queue looks stale (paused / launch-blocked / idle / saturated / blocked / stalled / running) for the Scheduler header, reusing classifyQueueStarvation so the header can never disagree with the watchdog. (3b99402 (2026-09-13) feat(scheduler): add a queue-health header naming why the queue looks stale)
- [structural] Wall-clock job budget kill (budgetKillTimer, scheduler.cjs:4976; MAX_JOB_DURATION_MS = 4h in schedulerConfig.cjs) bounds how long a stuck 'running' row can suppress that project's starvation verdict. (164184e feat(scheduler): add a wall-clock budget kill for runaway jobs (PRD 1204))
- [patch] dispatchPhase/dispatchPhaseAt breadcrumbs stamped across the dispatch region of spawnJob (scheduler.cjs:6020/6043/6085/6132/6189/6198), so a row stuck mid-dispatch is attributable post-hoc. (a23ceb8 fix(scheduler): stamp a dispatchPhase breadcrumb across the dispatch region)
- [structural] Reaper releases quietMachineLease for a vanished job (scheduler.cjs, inside reapDeadRunningJobs' mutate: `if (s.jobs[idx].quietMachine === true) quietMachineLease.release(slug)`) — closes the 'quiet lease held forever wedges all dispatch' path. (in-tree at HEAD (comment: 'a quietMachine job whose process silently vanished ... wedges the lease held forever'))

**Proposed structural fix (pre-verification):** Introduce ONE named, level-triggered reconcile loop that owns dispatch liveness, and demote every existing tick path to a mere hint. (a) New module `src/main/lib/dispatchLoop.cjs` exporting `startDispatchLoop({ tick, readState, now, intervalMs })`. It arms its own `setInterval(RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS = 30_000)` created at scheduler init and NEVER cleared by any other subsystem — not by setPaused, not by clearFireTimer, not by backoff. Its body is `try { ... } catch` end to end so it can never die from a throw, and it does not read `backoffMs`, `cachedUtilization`, `firePolicy`, or `cancelToken`. Every pass: re-read state from disk (queueStore.readMerged), compute the desired-vs-actual delta, and call tick(). Idempotent by construction — tickQueue already is (pickNextBatch + runningSet + sessionSlots make a redundant tick a no-op). (b) Make tickQueue un-wedgeable. Wrap the chained body in `Promise.race([body, timeout(TICK_WATCHDOG_MS = 120_000)])`, and on timeout log `[scheduler] TICK WEDGED`, append an audit event, and RESET `tickTail = Promise.resolve()` so the next reconcile pass starts a fresh chain. Apply the identical race to `mutateTail`. This is the single highest-value change
- files: src/main/lib/dispatchLoop.cjs (new), src/main/scheduler.cjs (tickQueue tickTail race + reset ~7264-7425; recordTick/lastProductiveTickAt ~2947-2949; runQueueStarvationWatchdog idle-clock swap ~7728-7740; heartbeatInterval body try/catch + payload ~10540-10612; startDispatchLoop call in init ~10520-10530; reapDeadRunningJobs trailing tick demoted to a hint ~8313), src/main/lib/queueStore.cjs (per-shard unreadableCwds + prefix recovery for shards; persist lastProductiveTickAt alongside lastDispatchAttemptAt), src/main/lib/sessionSlots.cjs (new reconcile(liveOwners) + holder age in snapshot), src/main/lib/quietMachineLease.cjs (reconcile against live slugs), src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs (evaluateDispatchLiveness + DISPATCH_DEAD_MS), scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs (third decision branch), src/main/health.cjs (read lastProductiveTickAt instead of lastDispatchAttemptAt in evaluateQueueDispatchHealth)
- tests: Pure-unit, no live state, no worktree sweep (the gitWorktree.test tmp-root hazard means every new test must use an injected root, never a default): - dispatchLoop: fake timers; assert tick() is called every intervalMs for N cycles while tick() rejects, throws synchronously, and returns a never-settl
- kill switch / migration: Env kill switches following the existing SM_SUPERVISOR_DISABLE / SM_REVERIFY_PERIODIC_DISABLE convention, all default-on: SM_DISPATCH_LOOP_DISABLE=1 (revert to today's edge-triggered behaviour exactly
- estimate: 180 min; risk: Medium-low, concentrated in three places. ### state durability and in-memory state

**Root-cause category:** Two system-of-record tiers with no contract between them: the disk tier is a multi-file, generation-less, best-effort write with per-incident point recovery bolted onto whichever file tore last, and the runtime tier (concurrency counters, leases, in-flight sets) is unpersisted module-level mutable state that is TRUSTED rather than re-derived. Every incident in this class is the same shape — a counter or latch that only ever moves in the unsafe direction, or a file whose parse failure is treated as a terminal fact instead of a repairable one — and each fix so far has hardened exactly the one va

**Current mechanism:** DISK MODEL (src/main/lib/queueStore.cjs, 647 lines). Federated since 6586edb: per-project job shards at `<cwd>/session-manager-operations/scheduler/state/queue.json` (path built by `projectStateDir`/`projectQueuePath`, fail-closed on relative / ops-internal / ephemeral cwd via `resolveOpsRoot(cwd,{opsInternal:'refuse'})`), per-project `history.jsonl`, and one machine file `MACHINE_STATE_PATH` = ~/.claude/session-manager/scheduler-machine.json holding config/scheduledFor/lastRunAt/lastDispatchAttemptAt/paused/launchBlocks/launchMitigations (`shapeMachine`, queueStore.cjs:223). A second sidecar `SCHEDULER_STATE_PATH` = ~/.claude/session-manager/scheduler-state.json holds poll-loop counters (`loadSchedulerState`/`persistSchedulerState`, scheduler.cjs:1471/1484) via config.writeJsonSync. WRITE PATH. `writeJsonAtomic`/`writeJsonAtomicSync` (queueStore.cjs:167/151) = mkdir → open `uniqueTmpPath(file)` (queueStore.cjs:144, `${file}.tmp-${pid}-${Date.now()}-${8 hex}` — per-CALL uniqueness, landed f56bdc0) → write → fsync → rename → unlink tmp on error. `writeSplit(state, defaultCwd)` (queueStore.cjs:533) writes the machine file FIRST, then one file per cwd in `state.sourceCwds ∪ {job.cwd}`; a per-shard write failure is logged and swallowed so other projects still land; ends with `bustCwdCache()`. READ PATH. `readMerged`/`readMergedSync` (queueStore.cjs:452/487) = `loadMachineState*` then loop `stateCwds(opts)` (30 s `cwdCache`, queueStore.cjs:194). Machine-file parse failure → `recoverTornMachineState*` (queueStore.cjs:349/362) → `buildMachineStateRecovery` → `findLongestValidJsonPrefix` (queueStore.cjs:261) keeps the longest balanced valid prefix, logs a `PAUSED_HINT_RE` warning if the discarded tail mentioned paused/resumeAt, persists the recovered object, and dispatch continues. Shard rows are validated row-by-row by `shapeJobs` (queueStore.cjs:422) against `ScheduleJobSchema`; a bad ROW is quarantined into `invalidJobs`, but a bad FILE (JSON.parse throw) sets the single top-level `out.unreadable`/`out.unreadablePath`. SCHEDULER LAYER. `readQueueSync`/`readQueue` (scheduler.cjs:2044/2053) merge DEFAULT_CONFIG and call `flagUnreadable` (scheduler.cjs:2017), which copies the file to `<path>.corrupt-<epoch>` once per PATH via the `quarantinedPaths` Set (scheduler.cjs:2016) and logs. `writeQueue` (scheduler.cjs:2059) refuses a state carrying `unreadable`. `mutate(fn)` (scheduler.cjs:2076) is the serialized read-modify-write: `mutateTail` promise chain (scheduler.cj

**Landed fixes:**
- [structural] uniqueTmpPath() — per-call pid+ts+random tmp name replaces the pid-only name that let two overlapping writes in the same process share one tmp and publish interleaved bytes; this was the root cause of the scheduler-machine.json tears (f56bdc0 "fix(scheduler): stop scheduler-machine.json torn-file corruption at the source" (2026-09-11 16:22 -0700), queue)
- [structural] findLongestValidJsonPrefix + buildMachineStateRecovery + recoverTornMachineState/Sync — a torn machine file recovers its longest valid JSON prefix, persists it, and keeps dispatching instead of halting on `unreadable`; PAUSED_HINT_RE makes an unrecoverable paused/resumeAt tail loud (f56bdc0, queueStore.cjs:261/315/349/362)
- [patch] quarantinedPaths Set replaced a module-level boolean latch so one file's tear no longer swallows every other file's .corrupt-* copy for the process's life (observed live 2026-09-11) (scheduler.cjs:2016 flagUnreadable)
- [structural] shapeJobs row-level quarantine against ScheduleJobSchema — one row with an out-of-enum status is isolated into invalidJobs instead of silently vanishing from every picker (2026-08-07 incident) (queueStore.cjs:422)
- [structural] reserveWorktreeSlot + withKindLock + getObservedWorktreeCount — an at-cap rejection is verified against on-disk `git worktree list` reality and the leaked counter corrected down, replacing the bare check+increment that made 5 missed decrements a machine-wide dispatch death until restart (gitWorktree.cjs:728/759, activeWorktreeCount at :185)
- [structural] mutate() serialized promise-tail writer + bail-before-fn on unreadable + isQueueRowRegression detection of a running→pending transition with a SHRUNK statusHistory (evidence of a write from a stale snapshot) (scheduler.cjs:2074-2126)
- [structural] instanceLock.acquireSchedulerOwnership — identity-independent `wx` pid lock at a fixed path, so a Playwright/dev Electron launched under a different userData can no longer run boot reconciliation against live state, SIGTERM live jobs and clobber admin-api.json (PRD 834, src/main/lib/instanceLock.cjs)
- [structural] reconcileQueueOffline deleted from the external watchdog; scheduler.cjs's partitionBootOrphans/applyOrphanOutcome became the single owner of orphan reconciliation, removing the second cross-process queue writer (PRD 686, watchdogHelpers.cjs:121 comment, scheduler.cjs:3384/3419)
- [structural] projectStateDir fail-closed chain: absolute-path required, refuse a cwd inside an ops root, refuse an ephemeral (tmpdir/linked-worktree) cwd via the single resolveOpsRoot resolver — stops writeSplit materializing stray/nested/ephemeral ops roots whose state is silently destroyed (7edc303 (2026-08-13), c1e1f21 (2026-08-30), 2dded49 + 5bd2964/PRD 1082 (2026-09-01), queueStore.cjs:62-104)
- [patch] lastDispatchAttemptAt added to shapeMachine and writeSplit so the starvation classifier's "engine is evaluating" signal stops being dropped on every read/write round trip (ecb1c10 (2026-09-12), queueStore.cjs:229/538)
- [patch] bustCwdCache() chains into activeSessions.bustProjectCwdCache so a brand-new project's first shard is visible immediately rather than after an independent TTL (queueStore.cjs:205)

DERIVED = `runningSet`, `activeWorktreeCount`, `sessionSlots.holders`, `quietMachineLease.heldBySlug`, `investigationsInFlight`, `deferredInvestigations` — never trusted across a boot, always rebuilt. (2) `reconcileRuntimeCounters()` in a new `src/main/lib/schedulerRuntimeState.cjs`: rebuilds every derived counter from (a) rows with status running/investigating plus their `runtime.pid`, (b) `gitWorktree.getObservedWorktreeCount` / `listCwdHolders`, (c) `instanceLock.pidAlive` on each recorded holder pid. ### post-run adjudication — why good work gets parked in needs_review

**Root-cause category:** Heuristic adjudication over an unreliable proxy: the scheduler decides "is this PRD done?" by pattern-matching the model's own transcript (is_error position, FAIL/Traceback regexes, a self-reported sentinel string) instead of executing the PRD's own machine-checkable acceptance gate and checking git for the commit. Because the proxy has no ground truth, every false positive can only be answered with another narrow exemption — 12+ exemptions now live in runVerify.cjs/commitGuardVerdict — and each exemption is a new surface for the next false positive. The same rootless adjudication then forces 

**Current mechanism:** see above

### executor process lifecycle vs the app process

**Root-cause category:** Executor lifetime is coupled to the app process because the only durable dispatch record is an asynchronously-written field in queue.json (`runtime.pid`), and every supervision responsibility — budget/idle/deadman enforcement, adoption, salvage, branch integration, finalize — lives in Electron main-process memory. A booting process therefore cannot distinguish "my run, still healthy, resume supervising it" from "abandoned orphan, terminalize it", so it defaults to KILL (pid known) or DOUBLE-RUN (pid unknown). Boot reconciliation is a fresh-start reconciler, not an adopter.

**Current mechanism:** SPAWN. `executeJob` (src/main/scheduler.cjs) builds four in-process watchdogs — `resultTailWatchdog`, `deadmanWatchdog` (`MAX_JOB_DURATION_MS` = 4h, src/main/lib/schedulerConfig.cjs), `idleTailWatchdog` (`IDLE_OUTPUT_KILL_MS` = 20min, scheduler.cjs), `budgetWatchdog` (`computeJobBudgetMs` = clamp(est × `JOB_BUDGET_FACTOR` 3, `JOB_BUDGET_FLOOR_MS` 45m, `JOB_BUDGET_CEILING_MS` 180m) + `shouldKillForBudget`) — and hands them to `withChildAndLog` (src/main/lib/childWithLog.cjs) with `spawn.options.detached: true`, cwd = worktree dir or project cwd. All four are `setInterval`s owned by the Electron main process. On spawn it synchronously writes `[scheduler] spawned pid=<n> sessionId=<id> (process group)` into the run log, calls `biasJobOomScore(child.pid)`, then ASYNCHRONOUSLY persists the pid via the `onPid` callback → `spawnJob`'s mutate: `s.jobs[idx].runtime = { pid, runId, startedAt, sessionId, cwd }` (one retry, then `appendAuditEvent('job_pid_persist_failed')`). DURABLE STATE AT DISPATCH. Only `queue.json`'s `runtime` + `guardBaseline`/`guardHeadBefore`/`preRunDirtyPaths`/`dispatchPhase` on the row. The run dir gets ONLY `<slug>.log`; `<slug>.meta.json` (`metaPath`) is written exclusively from `onExit` / early-exit paths. ### version drift and upgrade

**Root-cause category:** Build identity is resolved at runtime from the source tree (git) rather than baked into the artifact at publish time, and there is no drain-aware restart primitive — so the running code is unidentifiable from disk (sha null, version non-unique, heartbeat/health silent) and the only way to adopt new code is a destructive manual restart that kills live executors. Every symptom in this class is a consequence of "identity is inferred, not stamped" plus "restart is an event, not a state machine".

**Current mechanism:** LAUNCH PATH. The live app is the npx-cached build, not the repo. `ps` shows pid 2966462 `npm exec claude-code-session-manager@latest` → pid 2966482 `node /home/bilko/.npm/_npx/5346543b21849140/node_modules/.bin/claude-code-session-manager` → pid 2966490 `electron /home/bilko/.npm/_npx/5346543b21849140/node_modules/claude-code-session-manager` (etimes 26354s, started 2026-09-13 08:12:25). `bin/cli.cjs` is the whole launcher: it `require('electron')` and `spawn(electronBin, [appRoot])` where `appRoot = path.join(__dirname, '..')`, so `main: src/main/index.cjs` boots out of the npx cache. `scripts/postinstall.cjs` is in `files:` but does no version bookkeeping relevant to drift. WHAT IS LIVE VS REPO. `/home/bilko/.npm/_npx/5346543b21849140/node_modules/claude-code-session-manager/package.json` says version `0.86.0`; `/home/bilko/Projects/session-manager/package.json` says version `0.86.0`. The version strings are IDENTICAL and the code is not: npx `src/main/scheduler.cjs` is 10279 lines / 520660 bytes, repo `src/main/scheduler.cjs` is 11433 lines / 587618 bytes. I identified the running commit exactly: `diff <(git show 956839d:src/main/scheduler.cjs) <npx>/src/main/scheduler.cjs` is empty — the running build IS commit `956839d` ("chore(release): bump to v0.86.0", 2026-09-12 17:20:15). `git log 956839d..HEAD` = 54 commits, 32 of which touch `src/main/scheduler.cjs` or `src/main/lib/`. So the running engine is 54 commits / ~1150 scheduler lines behind HEAD, under the same version number. `diff -rq` of the two `src/main/lib` trees shows whole modules that do not exist in the running build at all: `needsReviewLedger.cjs`, `watchdogHelpers.cjs`, `activeSessions.cjs`, `bootSelfHeal.cjs`, `prdDisposition.cjs`, `guardShims.cjs`, `effectiveModelInfo.cjs`, plus differing `queueStore.cjs`, `reaperHelpers.cjs`, `gitWorktree.cjs`, `launchFailure.cjs`, `schedulerConfig.cjs`, `scheduleJobTransitions.cjs`. DOES RUN META RECORD THE SHA. Nominally yes, actually no. `src/main/scheduler.cjs` defines `SCHEDULER_BOOTED_AT` and `SCHEDULER_CODE_SHA` (line 184/188), and `SCHEDULER_CODE_SHA` is `execFileSync('git', ['-C', __dirname, 'rev-parse', '--short', 'HEAD'])` with `catch { return null }`. `__dirname` in production is the npx cache, which has no `.git` (`ls -a` shows bin/.claude-plugin/dist/node_modules/package.json/plugins/scripts/src only; `git -C <npx> rev-parse` → "fatal: not a git repository"). Both meta writers (`config.writeJsonSync(metaPath, …)` at the spawn-failure bran

So a restart is always destructive to in-flight work by design. — evidence: src/main/scheduler.cjs:10230-10243: `for (const slug of deferredSlugs) { … const result = killOrphanClaudePid(pid); … }`; executors spawned with `detached: true` at scheduler.cjs:5012; partitionBootOrphans at 3384 classifies live-pid rows as 'deferred'
- [parks-good-work, reproducible in HEAD] The only automatic relaunch is crash-triggered, never upgrade-triggered, and it resolves npm's latest dist-tag — which for this machine is the 54-commits-old 0.86.0. The watchdog can therefore 'successfully' restart the app straight back onto the stale build, with no signal that it did. — evidence: scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs main(): relaunch only on the `fresh === false` branch (DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS = 180_000); defaultSpawnRelaunch in src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs:206-222 spawns `npx claude-code-session-manager@latest`
- [kills-dispatch, reproducible in HEAD] rebootApp's relaunch races the single-instance lock: the child npx instance is spawned, then the parent waits only 400 ms before app.exit(0). If the child reaches requestSingleInstanceLock before the parent releases it, terminateLosingInstance exits the child and the app does not come back until the — evidence: src/main/index.cjs:348-362 (spawn, 400ms timer, then app.exit(0)); src/main/index.cjs:1062-1065 gotLock/terminateLosingInstance; src/main/lib/singleInstanceGuard.cjs:15-18 exits immediately on loss
- [parks-good-work, reproducible in HEAD] The repo→npm gap cannot be closed by any restart. A restart only moves the app from the npx cache copy to npm's latest; 54 unpublished commits stay unpublished. 'Restart onto new code' and 'ship the fix' are two different operations and neither is automatic. — evidence: npx build byte-identical to 956839d (diff of git show 956839d:src/main/scheduler.cjs against the npx file is empty); 54 commits since; publish is a manual skill (session-manager-dev:builder, package.json prepublishOnly)
- [silent, reproducible in HEAD] No build stamp is embedded at publish time. prepublishOnly runs build:project-pages, build:project-pages-logic, project-pages:gate, vite build — nothing writes the source sha into the tarball, which is the root reason the runtime sha resolution has nothing to read. — evidence: package.json scripts.prepublishOnly; package.json files[] lists no build-info artifact; no build-info/BUILD_SHA file under the npx install (ls -a shows bin, .claude-plugin, dist, node_modules, package.json, plugins, scripts, src, LICENSE, README.md, screenshots)

Resolution order: `build-info.json` (shipped artifact) → `git -C __dirname rev-parse` (dev checkout) → `null`. (d) Add `build: { version, codeSha, builtAt, appPath, bootedAt }` to the `appendHeartbe

**Open questions:**
- Should the publish itself be automated? The 54-commit repo→npm gap is the larger half of this class and no restart mechanism touches it. Today publishing is the manual session-manager-dev:builder skill. A drain-and-restart that always lands on a 54-commit-old build is a partial win; the question is 
- Does the executor-adoption work (reattach a detached `claude -p` across a restart by reading its JSONL and pid rather than SIGTERMing it) have an owner? Until it exists the drain must block on runningCount === 0, which with p90 60 min / max 240 min means an upgrade can legitimately wait hours. Adopt
- What is the right drain deadline? Observed max run is 240 min. A 4h deadline means an upgrade can be deferred a full working day if long jobs chain. Alternative: drain opportunistically at any natural runningCount===0 moment (the queue hits idle often) and never set a deadline at all — simpler and a
- Should `build-info.json` record the sha of the publishing checkout only, or also a content hash of src/main (so a dirty publish is detectable)? A `dirty: true` stamp is cheap and would have caught any hand-edit-in-the-npx-cache scenario.
- Does the npx cache ever get refreshed underneath a running app? The cache dir mtime is 2026-09-13 01:13 and the app booted 08:12, so on this machine running == on-disk installed. But if a second `npx @latest` refreshes the cache mid-run, 'installed on disk' and 'running' diverge inside the same dire
- Should the drain also gate on `investigating` and on the needs_review ladder's in-flight fix chains, or only on `running`? A restart mid-ladder may be as costly as a restart mid-run given the ladder's autoFixAttempted gating; the proposed evaluateDrain counts investigating as busy, but that is a jud
- The queue currently shows consecutiveFailures: 26 in the heartbeat alongside 0 failed rows in the last 30 days of history — worth confirming that counter is not itself a drift artifact of the stale running build before any of this is interpreted.

### test-time hazards against live scheduler state

**Root-cause category:** Hardcoded, duplicated homedir/tmpdir roots with no single resolver and no environment indirection — every module computes its own root at require time from `os.homedir()`/`os.tmpdir()`, so "which state am I touching" is decided by a constant rather than by configuration. Isolation is therefore per-test, voluntary, and invisible: the only lever is `process.env.HOME`, which does not reach the tmpdir-derived worktree roots at all, and there is nothing that can detect a test resolving a live root. (The project already solved exactly this shape once for project-relative state — `opsOwnership.cjs`'s

**Current mechanism:** Every scheduler root is a module-level `const` computed independently from `os.homedir()` / `os.tmpdir()` at require time, with ZERO env override anywhere (`grep -rn "SM_SCHEDULER_ROOT|SM_SCHEDULER_HOME|SM_WORKTREE_ROOT" src scripts tests` returns nothing). Roots, one per module, no shared resolver: - `ROOT` (src/main/scheduler.cjs:898) = `path.join(os.homedir(), '.claude','session-manager','scheduled-plans')`; also `SCHEDULER_STATE_PATH` (:903) and `HEARTBEAT_PATH` (:904). - `ROOT` (src/main/queueOps.cjs:43) — a SECOND independent copy of the same scheduled-plans literal. - `MACHINE_STATE_PATH` + `LEGACY_QUEUE_PATH` (src/main/lib/queueStore.cjs:40-41), plus a hardcoded `MACHINE_STATE_LOG_CWD = path.join(os.homedir(),'Projects','session-manager')` (:294). - `CONFIG_PATH` for session-slots-config.json (src/main/lib/sessionSlots.cjs:35). - `KIND_CONFIG` (src/main/lib/gitWorktree.cjs:87-99): `job` root `path.join(os.tmpdir(),'session-manager-job-worktrees')`, `epic` root `.../session-manager-epic-worktrees`, read via `worktreeRootFor(kind)` (:114). NOT affected by `$HOME`. - `TOKEN_PATH` admin-api.json (src/main/lib/localAdminHttp.cjs:41) — the ONLY root with an override: `SM_ADMIN_TOKEN_PATH`, plus `SM_DEV=1`/`SM_E2E=1` variants in `tokenPath()` (:55-67). This is the existing precedent and proof the pattern works. - Per-project queue shards: `projectStateDir`/`projectQueuePath`/`queuePathOrSkip` (queueStore.cjs:100-129) resolve through `opsOwnership.cjs`'s `resolveProjectRoot` + `OPS_ROOT_DIR` (:35) — "THE ONE ops-root resolver (PRD 1082)" — which already has an `assertOpsWrite` ephemeral-cwd refusal (opsOwnership.cjs:170-193, via `ephemeralCwd.cjs`'s `isEphemeralCwd`). Test-side: `vitest.config.ts` has NO `globalSetup`, NO `setupFiles`, NO `env` block — only `environment:'node'`, `testTimeout`, a hand-maintained 250-entry `include` array, and `globals:true`. Nothing sets HOME or any root for the suite. No module under src/main checks `process.env.VITEST`/`NODE_ENV` (`grep -rn "VITEST|NODE_ENV" src/main/lib/gitWorktree.cjs src/main/scheduler.cjs src/main/lib/queueStore.cjs src/main/queueOps.cjs src/main/lib/sessionSlots.cjs` → empty). Protection is per-test and voluntary: some tests set `process.env.HOME = tmpHome` before `require('../scheduler.cjs')` (e.g. scheduler-worktree-cap-defer.test.cjs beforeAll), 14 tests that touch `os.homedir()` do not. `CLAUDE.md:38` instructs every executor to run `npm run test:unit` (= `vitest run`), and jobs run with cwd = th

### observability and health signals (scheduler)

**Root-cause category:** Snapshot-derived health with no durable event stream and no automatic evaluator. Every signal is recomputed from CURRENT mutable state (queue.json rows, an in-memory counter, a transient read flag, a once-per-streak boolean) by a cold, hand-run process that loads REPO code rather than the running build — so anything that self-heals, resets its own flag, or happens between two human invocations is unobservable by construction, and the one process that does run automatically (the 2-minute watchdog) supervises a single liveness bit (heartbeat freshness) that is emitted by a timer decoupled from t

**Current mechanism:** FIVE disconnected surfaces, none of which is automatic and none of which is derived from a durable event stream. (1) `src/main/health.cjs` — a COLD CLI (`package.json` script "health" → `node src/main/health.cjs`, wrapped by `scripts/health.sh`). `check()` builds `status.components` for: nodejs, typescript, build_artifact, test_infrastructure, config_dir, `scheduler_queue` (via `evaluateTickLiveness` + `computeStallSummary` + `evaluatePerProjectStall` + `computeProjectProblemCounts` + `evaluateWorktreeCapBlocked`), `queue_dispatch` (via `evaluateQueueDispatchHealth` → `classifyQueueStarvation`, threshold `DISPATCH_STALL_THRESHOLD_MS` = 2h), `project_starve_escalation` (via `evaluateStarveEscalationHealth` + `findStarvedProjects` + `latestStarveEscalationReasons`), `usage_poller` (`loadUsagePollerState` + `evaluateUsagePollerHealth`, threshold `FAILURE_STREAK_WARN_THRESHOLD`=5), prd_migration, transcripts_dir, app_logs, `claude_md_budget`, persona_imports, `delegation_chain`, run_log_retention, `epic_index`. `criticalComponents` gates `status.ok`. Every component is recomputed from CURRENT mutable state; nothing reads a history of what happened. (2) `appendHeartbeat(entry)` in `src/main/scheduler.cjs` (~L1509), writing `HEARTBEAT_PATH` = ~/.claude/session-manager/scheduler-heartbeat.log, rotated at `HEARTBEAT_MAX_BYTES` (1 MiB) to `.log.1`. Fired from a bare `setInterval(..., 60_000)` (~L10608) INDEPENDENT of dispatch. Fields: `ts, pid, counts{pending,running,investigating,completed,skipped,failed,needs_review,quarantined,unknown}, stall{stalled,total}, paused, nextReset, utilization, consecutiveFailures`. Read back by `readFreshHeartbeat` (health, `HEARTBEAT_STALE_MS`=5 min) and by `readLastHeartbeatTs` (watchdog). (3) `~/.claude/session-manager/audit-log.jsonl` via `appendAuditEvent(kind, fields)` in `src/main/lib/auditLog.cjs`. Open-set `kind` string, no rotation (bare `fs.appendFileSync`). 26 distinct kinds emitted from scheduler.cjs; the richest is `job_transition` / `job_transition_refused`, emitted inside `transitionJob` in `src/main/lib/scheduleJobTransitions.cjs` — the single choke point where `job.status` is assigned (also pushes `statusHistory`, capped `STATUS_HISTORY_CAP`=20, and calls `telemetryCounters.trackSchedulerJobFinish` for `FINISH_STATUSES`). Only ONE health rule reads this log: `latestStarveEscalationReasons`. (4) `src/main/lib/queueHealth.cjs` `computeQueueHealth(jobs)` (`STUCK_STATUSES` = needs_review|quarantined) → `runQueueHealthS

### path/project resolution and hot-path cost

**Root-cause category:** Project identity is re-derived from the filesystem on every call instead of being owned by one cached, event-invalidated registry — and every derivation step fails OPEN. Three modules (activeSessions.projectRootOf, ephemeralCwd.isEphemeralCwd, opsOwnership.parseOpsPath) each re-implement "what project is this path", disagree (indexOf vs lastIndexOf), and each returns "ordinary project root" when it cannot prove otherwise, so an unprovable worktree becomes a writable project. The invariant that PRD 1082 established structurally is then policed by a lint that only a human can run, so drift is de

**Current mechanism:** Three layers, all synchronous, all re-derived from the filesystem per call. (1) DISCOVERY — `src/main/lib/activeSessions.cjs` (moved out of `scripts/lib/` by commit d1edf15; `scripts/lib/` no longer exists in the repo). `activeProjectCwds(maxAgeMin, {projectsDir, maxCwds, tmpDropRoots})` does `readdirSync(~/.claude/projects)` (2209 entries live), per-dir `readdirSync` + `statSync` per `*.jsonl`, then a 64 KB tail read (`readTailLines`, `TAIL_BYTES`) of the newest transcript, and feeds `row.cwd` into the inner `addCwd`. `addCwd` normalizes via `projectRootOf` (ops-dir truncation on `OPS_DIRNAME`, then `worktreeMainRootOf`), then applies the tmp-drop guards `TMP_DROP_ROOTS.exactDropCwds` (os.tmpdir() exact) and `TMP_DROP_ROOTS.prefixDropRoots` (the two `gitWorktree.KIND_CONFIG` job/epic roots). `allProjectCwds(opts)` = `activeProjectCwds(Infinity, {maxCwds:500, ...opts})`. `worktreeMainRootOf` walks up `MAX_WORKTREE_WALK` levels, parses the `.git` file's `gitdir:` line, and requires a round-trip check against `<main>/.git/worktrees/<name>/gitdir` before accepting. A cache landed TODAY (commit 7ce4130, 2026-09-13 09:15 PDT): `cwdScanCache` keyed on `[projectsDir,maxAgeMin,maxCwds].join('|')`, invalidated by `statSync(projectsDir).mtimeMs` + `CACHE_TTL_MS = 30_000`, busted only by `bustProjectCwdCache()`. (2) OPS-PATH CHOKE POINT — `src/main/lib/opsOwnership.cjs` exports `OPS_ROOT_DIR`, `resolveProjectRoot(cwd,{opsInternal})`, `resolveOpsRoot(cwd,opts)`, `opsPath(cwd,...segments)` (landed by commit 5bd2964, PRD 1082). `checkOpsWrite`/`assertOpsWrite` additionally refuse an ephemeral project root. `src/main/lib/ephemeralCwd.cjs` `isEphemeralCwd` refuses via `isExactlyTmpdir` / `isUnderManagedWorktreeRoot` / `isLinkedWorktreeRoot`. (3) MCP BOUNDARY — `src/main/lib/projectRootResolve.cjs` `resolveProjectContext({cwd, originClaudeSessionId, epicId, originProjectRoot})` (commit 47936fb, PRD 1081) normalizes through `projectRootOf`, prefers a trusted `SM_PROJECT_ROOT` hint (stamped by `chatRunner.cjs:526`, `pty.cjs:173`, `scheduler.cjs:4828`; forwarded by `scripts/scheduler-mcp-server.cjs:527`), then searches every project from `allProjectCwds()` via `readActiveIndex` and lets `epicCwd` win. CONSUMERS — `queueStore.stateCwds` (own `CACHE_MS = 30_000` `cwdCache`, `bustCwdCache` chains to `bustProjectCwdCache`), `prdLocations.resolvePrdsDirs`/`resolveArchivedPrdsDirs` via `memoizedAssembledDirs`/`epicsRootsFreshnessKey`/`ASSEMBLED_DIRS_CACHE_TTL_MS`, `queueHistory.his

### fix-commit archaeology — quantitative bucket histogram of every scheduler fix/feat commit since 2026

**Root-cause category:** Heuristic adjudication with unbounded add-a-guard remediation: the scheduler decides job outcome from indirect, re-derivable evidence (transcript `is_error` scan, repo-wide `git log --since/--until` window, pid liveness, in-memory counters) rather than a per-job ground-truth receipt, and every false verdict has been patched by ADDING another predicate/exemption/rung (8 of the 10 largest fixes are near-pure additions, median 27 deletions) instead of replacing the evidence source. The recurrence is structural: each new guard creates a new eligibility gap between itself and the neighbouring guard

**Current mechanism:** SCOPE MEASURED: `git log --since=2026-08-01` over the 14 named paths returns 149 commits (the broader `src/main/lib/` + `src/main/queueOps.cjs` + `src/main/supervisor.cjs` sweep returns 224). Note `scripts/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs` NO LONGER EXISTS — d1edf15 ("refactor(main): move scripts/lib helpers into src/main/lib", 2026-09-13) moved it to `/home/bilko/Projects/session-manager/src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs`; `scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs` now requires `../src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs`. That move landed as commit d1edf15 and is the SAME commit recorded as `landedCommit` on the live parked row 1218-fo-01. **Landed fixes:**
- [structural] Dispatch driver independent of the billing poller: pollLoop's auth/transient/outer-catch branches now call maybeLaunchWhenAvailable; new lastDispatchAttemptAt stamped by tickQueue at the picker; starvation watchdog measures idleness from it (not lastRunAt) and resets the wedged cancelToken before fo (f18e161 (2026-09-11), field-carrying fix ecb1c10 (2026-09-12))
- [structural] Atomic writes + longest-valid-JSON-prefix recovery for scheduler-machine.json; unrecoverable file falls back to defaults 'so dispatch does not silently halt' rather than marking unreadable (f56bdc0 (2026-09-11))
- [structural] Worktree cap self-heals against observed on-disk state: on rejection, clamp activeWorktreeCount down to getObservedWorktreeCount before honoring the refusal; health.cjs evaluateWorktreeCapBlocked surfaces it (2cc28e1 (2026-09-11), preceded by 9093222 (defer instead of in-place fallback))
- [structural] Per-project starvation detection (machine-wide runningSet no longer disarms every project's watchdog) + bounded starve escalation with a per-cwd one-shot latch (2d876b9, a143552, classifyQueueStarvationByProject)
- [structural] Bounded automatic ladders replacing human intervention: failed auto-reset (0236c0c), exhausted needs_review auto-resolve with NEEDS_REVIEW_RESOLVE_CAP=2 (d3a799d), quarantine bounded exit + dead-end-status guard (d3941f4), guard-parked rows admitted to the same ladder (GUARD_VERDICT_EVIDENCE_ELIGIBL (0236c0c, d3a799d, d3941f4, bbb4373)

- [structural] Wall-clock job budget kill for runaway jobs (computeJobBudgetMs / shouldKillForBudget / resolveBudgetKillOutcome / classifyBudgetKill, SM_JOB_BUDGET_* env overrides) (164184e (PRD 1204, 2026-09-13))
- [structural] Launch circuit breaker: a run that never got a turn is not a failed PRD (launchFailure.cjs, 357 new lines) (2d2c415 (issue #11, 2026-09-02))

## Synthesis

**Ordering rationale:** Four independent chains fan out from one two-PRD foundation, so foundations land before anything that could be tested dangerously or reasoned about wrongly. FOUNDATION (1-3). sched-paths-resolver must be first because every other PRD needs somewhere safe to write and test: it is the only place a machine-wide root is derived, and until it exists no test can be isolated from the live queue or the live worktree roots (HOME never reached the tmpdir-derived KIND_CONFIG roots). test-sandbox-guard immediately follows because the very act of running `npm run test:unit` from a job worktree today deletes live worktrees (gitWorktree.test's four `staleAgeMs: 0` sweeps, two with an empty holders Set) — that hazard must be closed before 16 PRDs' worth of new tests get written by headless executors running in worktrees alongside live jobs. path-literal-lint then makes both invariants self-policing so PRD 110 cannot reintroduce the literal. IDENTITY (4-5). build-identity-stamp comes before every behavioural PRD because the running build is currently unidentifiable (version 0.86.0 covers at least two materially different trees, schedulerCodeSha is structurally null under npx), and every conclusion drawn from live state is unreliable until it is stamped. heartbeat-build-health puts that identity where the watchdog and health can read it and is the prerequisite for the external watchdog gaining any new decision. DURABILITY THEN LIVENESS (6-11). shard-quarantine-single-writer precedes the dispatch loop because a loop that ticks every 30 s against a machine-wide `unreadable` latch just spins; the write-path discipline in the same PRD also removes the stale-snapshot read the loop would otherwise amplify. tick-wedge-selfheal precedes dispatch-reconcile-loop deliberately — arming a fixed-cadence driver on top of an unbounded promise chain means every new tick queues behind the wedge. productive-tick-clock and runtime-counter-reconcile both hang off the loop: the clock makes a permanently-gated queue visible, the reconcile pass makes the gates that cause it self-healing, and both must exist before watchdog-dispatch-liveness can act on the difference between "process alive" and "dispatch alive". PROCESS IDENTITY THEN ADOPTION THEN DEPLOYMENT (12-15). proc-identity-lock supplies the identity triple that both the supervisor record and the hardened lock need. job-supervisor-record must bake through one restart before boot-adopt-executors flips the boot path, or adoption would rely on 

**Deliberately not built:**
- A machine-wide scheduler-events.jsonl ledger with a closed-set CLASSES enum plus a schedulerSlo.cjs evaluator (observability report). Rejected: it is a FOURTH event stream alongside audit-log.jsonl (87k lines), history.jsonl and the heartbeat, and every emit site it proposes is a chokepoint that already emits (transitionJob, the reap path, starve escalation). The same visibility is bought in heartbeat-build-health and watchdog-dispatch-liveness by extending the heartbeat payload and rotating/tai
- An out-of-process executor budget sweep in scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs that KILLS processes past maxDurationMs (executor-lifecycle report). Rejected: it hands machine-wide kill authority to a plain-node systemd timer, which is precisely the blast radius that made boot reconciliation destructive in the first place. boot-adopt-executors re-arms the budget in-process for adopted runs and watchdog-dispatch-liveness restores a dead app, so the only uncovered case is 'the app never comes back at al
- The full projectRegistry.cjs rewrite with a chokidar watcher over ~/.claude/projects (path-resolution report). Rejected: the scan cache already landed (7ce4130) and measures 300 ms cold / 0 ms warm over 2209 dirs, so the residual is one boot-time scan; a watcher on a 2209-entry directory is a larger regression risk than the cost it removes. Only the two cheap parts — frozen cache copies and a TTL above the poll cadence — are kept, inside cwd-classify-failclosed.
- Generation-stamped optimistic concurrency plus write-then-verify read-back on every shard write (state-durability report). Rejected: it roughly doubles read I/O on the mutate hot path to detect a second writer, when the actual second writers are two known code paths. shard-quarantine-single-writer removes the out-of-mutate writeQueue call and proc-identity-lock stops a recycled pid from pinning ownership — the causes rather than the symptom.
- Rotating .bak- snapshots plus restore-from-backup for torn state (state-durability report). Rejected outright: restoring a stale backup resurrects rows that already reached a terminal state — the 2026-07-31 '189 completed jobs resurrected' incident. Longest-valid-prefix recovery generalised to shards has no resurrection mode, which is why it is the only recovery mechanism in the chain.
- gate_red as a new park reason in the first pass (adjudication report). Deliberately deferred, not rejected: a flaky or environment-sensitive suite would newly park good work, which is the exact 1218-fo-01 failure shape the gate exists to fix. gate-green-unparks ships the un-park direction only; a red gate keeps falling through to today's chain until shadow-mode data shows the gate is stable.
- Automatic npm publish gated on typecheck/tests (version-drift report). Rejected as automatic: it is the only action in the set whose blast radius extends off this machine. drain-upgrade-restart restarts onto whatever npm `latest` resolves and reports the repo-ahead-of-npm gap as 'publish-needed', explicitly distinguished from 'restart-needed' so the two are never conflated; publishing stays human-gated.
- Letting reconcileRuntimeCounters kill a process it finds holding a slot with no matching queue row. Rejected: it repeats the boot-orphan hazard from the other side. The reconciler releases accounting only and never signals a process; an orphan with no row is the reaper's and the budget's problem, both of which already have evidence-before-failure gates.
- Extracting spawnJob's finally into a shared runPostRunPipeline so adopted runs can finalize themselves (executor-lifecycle report). Rejected for this chain: reapDeadRunningJobs already terminalizes a vanished running row with salvage and landedCommit evidence, so adoption reuses it. The extraction touches the most incident-dense region in the file (worktree integration, salvage, commit guard, verify) for no behaviour a reused path does not already provide.
- A second ops-root normalizer inside scripts/hooks/guard-inline-implementation.cjs. Rejected: the hook's raw agent-supplied cwd is exactly the input that needs the shared classifier, so it calls classifyCwd rather than growing a third implementation — the divergence between indexOf and lastIndexOf is the bug being deleted.
- Blocking DISPATCH on a cwd that classifies 'unknown'. Rejected: refusing ops WRITES is the safe half of fail-closed; refusing dispatch would trade a data-placement bug for a kills-dispatch bug, which is the failure mode the operator has been burned by most often.

### sched-paths-resolver — One lazy resolver for every machine-wide scheduler root (schedulerPaths.cjs)

- classes: test-time hazards against live scheduler state, path/project resolution, state durability; estimate 30 min; depends on: none
- problem: Every machine-wide root is a module-scope `const` computed from os.homedir()/os.tmpdir() at require time, duplicated across modules, with no env indirection: `ROOT` (src/main/scheduler.cjs:898), `SCHEDULER_STATE_PATH` (:903), `HEARTBEAT_PATH` (:904), a SECOND scheduled-plans literal in src/main/queueOps.cjs, `MACHINE_STATE_PATH`/`LEGACY_QUEUE_PATH` (queueStore.cjs:40-41), the hardcoded `MACHINE_STATE_LOG_CWD = ~/Projects/session-manager` (queueStore.cjs:294), `CONFIG_PATH` (sessionSlots.cjs:35) and `KIND_CONFIG` (gitWorktree.cjs:87). `grep -rn SM_SCHEDULER_ROOT src scripts` returns nothing, so nothing — test, self-e2e or job — can be pointed away from live state, and `process.env.HOME` (the only lever) does not reach the tmpdir-derived worktree roots at all.
- mechanism (as synthesized): New plain-Node `src/main/lib/schedulerPaths.cjs` (no electron require — it is loaded by queueStore.cjs, which must stay electron-free). Exports LAZY functions, never module-scope consts, so require order can never freeze a root: `schedulerHome()` (SM_SCHEDULER_HOME else ~/.claude/session-manager), `scheduledPlansRoot()`, `runsDir()`, `machineStatePath()`, `legacyQueuePath()`, `schedulerStatePath()`, `heartbeatPath()`, `sessionSlotsConfigPath()`, `adminTokenPath()` (absorbs localAdminHttp.cjs's existing SM_ADMIN_TOKEN_PATH / SM_DEV / SM_E2E ladder verbatim), `worktreeRoot(kind)` (SM_WORKTREE_ROOT else os.tmpdir()/session-manager-<kind>-worktrees), `machineStateLogCwd()` (SM_SCHEDULER_LOG_CWD else the resolved project root — deletes the hardcoded ~/Projects/session-manager). No behaviour change with no env set: every resolver returns the byte-identical path the old const returned.

### heartbeat-build-health — Heartbeat carries build identity, survives a throw, and health stops reading a 28 MB log whole

- classes: observability, version drift and upgrade, dispatch liveness; estimate 25 min; depends on: build-identity-stamp
- problem: appendHeartbeat (src/main/scheduler.cjs:1509) emits only {ts,pid,counts,stall,paused,nextReset,utilization,consecutiveFailures} — no build identity — so the one file the external watchdog and every operator read cannot answer which code is running. The heartbeat interval body (scheduler.cjs:10540) has no try/catch, so one throw from readQueueSync silently skips appendHeartbeat, the stall detector and runQueueStarvationWatchdog together for that minute while the app still looks alive. health.cjs `latestStarveEscalationReasons` (health.cjs:474) readFileSync's the whole audit-log.jsonl, which is 27.8 MB / 87k lines because src/main/lib/auditLog.cjs appendAuditEvent has no rotation (contrast appendHeartbeat's HEARTBEAT_MAX_BYTES rotate).
- mechanism (as synthesized): (1) appendHeartbeat's payload gains `build: {version, codeSha, builtAt, appPath}` from resolveBuildIdentity(). (2) The heartbeatInterval body (scheduler.cjs:10540) is wrapped end-to-end in try/catch that still writes a degraded heartbeat line ({ts,pid,degraded:true,error}) on throw, so a repeating throw can never silently kill the heartbeat, the stall detector and the starvation watchdog at once. (3) auditLog.cjs gains `AUDIT_MAX_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024` rotation to audit-log.jsonl.1 (copying appendHeartbeat's rotate-on-append pattern) and a bounded `readTail(nLines)`; health.cjs's latestStarveEscalationReasons switches to auditLog.readTail(2000). (4) health.cjs gains `evaluateBuildFreshness({running, installedOnDisk, repoHead})` and a `components.build` entry distinguishing 'restart-needed' (installed-on-disk sha differs from running sha) from 'publish-needed' (the app's own checkout is ahead of the published sha) so the two drift axes are never conflated; it is reported but deliberately NOT added to health.cjs:951 criticalComponents, so a publish gap cannot turn the whole report red.
- files: src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/lib/auditLog.cjs, src/main/health.cjs, src/main/__tests__/scheduler-heartbeat-payload.test.cjs, src/main/__tests__/health-build-freshness.test.cjs, src/main/lib/__tests__/auditLog.test.cjs

### shard-quarantine-single-writer — A torn project shard quarantines one project, not the machine — and writeQueue gets exactly one caller

- classes: state durability and in-memory state, dispatch liveness; estimate 30 min; depends on: test-sandbox-guard
- problem: src/main/lib/queueStore.cjs sets ONE top-level `out.unreadable` from any shard read error (readMergedSync:476, readMerged:509); scheduler.cjs then refuses every project: mutate() throws 'queue mutation skipped: queue.json unreadable', tickQueue returns {fired:false,reason:'unreadable'} (scheduler.cjs:7276) and reconcile is skipped — a machine-wide, latching halt with no auto-repair, while the machine file got findLongestValidJsonPrefix/buildMachineStateRecovery (queueStore.cjs:261/318) precisely 'so dispatch does not silently halt'. Separately the serialized-writer law has two holes: `schedule:adopt-prd` calls readQueue → reconcile → writeQueue OUTSIDE mutate (the only writeQueue call outside mutate), and tickQueue reads `state` once (7266) then performs two mutate() writes and calls reconcile(state) on that stale snapshot before pickNextBatch (7315).
- mechanism (as synthesized): (1) Generalize buildMachineStateRecovery into `recoverTornJson(raw, parseError, {label})` in queueStore.cjs (still electron-free, still using its own writeJsonAtomic/uniqueTmpPath) and call it for shards as well as MACHINE_STATE_PATH. (2) Replace the shard branch of `out.unreadable` with `out.unreadableCwds: [{cwd, file, error}]`; reserve the global `unreadable` for the machine file alone. (3) scheduler.cjs: mutate() bails only on machine-file unreadable; tickQueue and reconcile skip only rows whose cwd ∈ unreadableCwds; flagUnreadable keeps its per-path quarantinedPaths behaviour. (4) Single writer: drop writeQueue from module.exports and leave exactly one call site inside mutate; rewrite the schedule:adopt-prd handler as `await mutate(async (s) => { await reconcile(s); })`; move tickQueue's reconcile inside a mutate and re-read `state` after it so pickNextBatch never runs on a snapshot that predates two completed writes.

### tick-wedge-selfheal — tickTail/mutateTail can no longer wedge dispatch until a restart

- classes: dispatch liveness; estimate 20 min; depends on: test-sandbox-guard
- problem: `tickTail` is an unbounded promise chain with no timeout and no reset: `let tickTail = Promise.resolve()` (src/main/scheduler.cjs:7264), `const next = tickTail.then(...)` (:7270), `tickTail = next.catch(()=>{})` (:7424) — grep shows only those three references. `mutateTail` (:2074) has the identical shape. If one tick body never settles (a hung reconcile, a hung mutate, a hung dep-history git walk), EVERY subsequent tickQueue — including the starvation watchdog's forced tick — queues behind it forever. Nothing in the process or the external watchdog can break this; only an app restart can, and restarting has historically killed live detached executors.
- mechanism (as synthesized): Wrap the chained bodies of tickQueue (scheduler.cjs:7269-7424) and mutate (:2076) in `Promise.race([body, timeoutAfter(ms)])` with TICK_WATCHDOG_MS = 120_000 and MUTATE_WATCHDOG_MS = 60_000 (env overrides SM_TICK_WATCHDOG_MS / SM_MUTATE_WATCHDOG_MS; 0 disables the race and restores today's unbounded chain). On timeout: console.warn('[scheduler] TICK WEDGED'), appendAuditEvent('tick_wedged'|'mutate_wedged', {reason, elapsedMs, lastTickReason}), return recordTick({fired:false, reason:'wedged'}), and RESET `tickTail = Promise.resolve()` / `mutateTail = Promise.resolve()` so the next call starts a fresh chain. Safe because tickQueue is already idempotent — pickNextBatch filters runningSet, sessionSlots.acquire is the hard gate, and an already-completed slug is refused re-dispatch (52511b8).
- files: src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/__tests__/scheduler-tick-wedge.test.cjs, vitest.config.ts
- deletes/simplifies: Converts the only unrecoverable-without-restart dispatch failure into a 2-minute self-heal, removing 'restart the app' as a required recovery step.
- memories retired: 'dispatch has no driver — wedged cancelToken' — the cancelToken half was retired by f18e161; this retires its successor, 'a wedged tick chain stalls dispatch un

Specifically: (1) `tickTail` has exactly three references — the initialiser `let tickTail = Promise.resolve()` (line 7286), `const next = tickTail.then(async () => {` inside `tickQueue` (7292), and `tickTail = next.catch(() => {})` (7446); `grep -c 'tickTail = Promise.resolve()' src/main/scheduler.cjs` → 1, i.e. no reset path exists. (2) `mutateTail` has the identical shape: `let mutateTail = Promise.resolve()` (2074), `const next = mutateTail.then(async () => {` in `mutate(fn)` (2077), `mutateTail = next.catch(() => {})` (2124); `grep -c` → 1. ### dispatch-reconcile-loop — One named level-triggered dispatch loop owns cadence; delete the reaper's side-effect tick

- classes: dispatch liveness; estimate 25 min; depends on: tick-wedge-selfheal, shard-quarantine-single-writer
- problem: HEAD has no unconditional fixed-cadence dispatch tick. All 7 tickQueue call sites are edge-triggered, and the de-facto level tick is `tickQueue().catch(()=>{})` as the LAST statement inside reapDeadRunningJobs' try (src/main/scheduler.cjs), silently skipped whenever anything earlier in that ~380-line body throws. Cadence is the billing poller's backoff: pollLoop's finally uses `backoffMs || POLL_INTERVAL_MS` and nextBackoffMs doubles to BACKOFF_MAX_MS = 480_000, so an unhealthy usage endpoint degrades the dispatch tick from once/60 s to once/8 min — observed live with consecutiveFailures:27, backoffMs:480000 and 26 pending rows. maybeLaunchWhenAvailable additionally returns immediately unless firePolicy === 'when-available' (scheduler.cjs:7480).
- mechanism (as synthesized): New `src/main/lib/dispatchLoop.cjs` exporting `startDispatchLoop({tick, intervalMs, onError})` → {stop}: it arms its OWN setInterval (RECONCILE_INTERVAL_MS = 30_000, env SM_DISPATCH_LOOP_INTERVAL_MS clamped to [10_000, 300_000]), is never cleared by setPaused/clearFireTimer/backoff, has a try/catch around the whole body so it cannot die from a throw or an unsettled promise, and reads none of backoffMs / cachedUtilization / firePolicy / cancelToken. scheduler.cjs init() arms it with `tick: () => tickQueue()` (idempotent by construction, and now timeout-bounded by tick-wedge-selfheal). DELETE the trailing `tickQueue().catch(()=>{})` from reapDeadRunningJobs — the loop owns cadence, so the reaper stops being an accidental driver. Kill switch SM_DISPATCH_LOOP_DISABLE=1 simply never arms the loop, restoring today's edge-triggered behaviour exactly.

Cadence IS the billing poller's backoff. `pollLoop` (8355) re-arms in its `finally` with `const delay = backoffMs || POLL_INTERVAL_MS;` (8500). `BACKOFF_MAX_MS =

### productive-tick-clock — A no-op tick must stop refreshing the starvation watchdog's own idle clock

- classes: dispatch liveness, observability; estimate 25 min; depends on: dispatch-reconcile-loop
- problem: tickQueue stamps `s.lastDispatchAttemptAt` at src/main/scheduler.cjs:7290 — immediately after the unreadable/paused guards and BEFORE the slots gate (:7327), pickNextBatch holds (:7337), the memory gate (:7370) and the load gate (:7408). classifyQueueStarvation (:7528) computes idleMs from that stamp and returns null while idleMs < QUEUE_STARVATION_MS (:7499, 10 min), so a queue that ticks forever and dispatches nothing — a leaked slot, a stale hold, a stuck gate — looks healthy to the watchdog permanently. This is the pre-f18e161 'lastRunAt disarms the watchdog' bug structurally reproduced one field over. health.cjs's own DISPATCH_STALL_THRESHOLD_MS is 2 h (health.cjs:418), so the cold report cannot catch it either.
- mechanism (as synthesized): recordTick (scheduler.cjs:2948) additionally stamps `lastProductiveTickAt` ONLY for outcomes where not launching is correct — fired, drained, already-running, blocked — and never for slots-exhausted / held / memory-deferred / load-deferred / unreadable / wedged. queueStore.shapeMachine (:223) and writeSplit (:533) carry the new field so it survives every read-write cycle (the same fix ecb1c10 had to make for lastDispatchAttemptAt). classifyQueueStarvation (:7528) and classifyQueueStarvationByProject (:7573) take their idle clock from lastProductiveTickAt; runQueueStarvationWatchdog (:7728) passes it; health.cjs evaluateQueueDispatchHealth (:433) reads it and DISPATCH_STALL_THRESHOLD_MS drops from 2 h to 15 min (QUEUE_STARVATION_MS + margin) and is exported so the threshold is assertable. lastDispatchAttemptAt keeps its existing meaning — 'the loop is alive' — and is what the heartbeat/watchdog use to tell 'loop dead' from 'loop alive but unproductive'.
- files: src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/lib/queueStore.cjs, src/main/health.cjs, src/main/__tests__/queue-starvation-dispatch-driver.test.cjs, src/main/__tests__/health-queue-dispatch.test.cjs
- deletes/simplifies: Removes the structural repeat of a bug already fixed once (an idle clock that every no-op refreshes) instead of adding a second watchdog to compensate for it; collapses health.cjs's 2 h dispatch SLO onto the same 10-minute verdict the in-app watchdog already uses.

(3) recordTick (scheduler.cjs:2970) only assigns the in-memory `lastTick` object; it persists nothing and stamps no clock. (4) classifyQueueStarvation (:7550) computes `idleMs

### runtime-counter-reconcile — Re-derive every in-memory concurrency counter from disk on each loop pass

- classes: state durability and in-memory state, dispatch liveness; estimate 30 min; depends on: dispatch-reconcile-loop
- problem: Only ONE derived counter re-derives itself from reality: gitWorktree.reserveWorktreeSlot (src/main/lib/gitWorktree.cjs:759) clamps activeWorktreeCount down to getObservedWorktreeCount when already at cap. Its siblings do not. sessionSlots.cjs's `holders` Map carries no pid and has no sweep (module.exports is acquire/release/subscribe/snapshot/setCap/__resetForTests only) — a token stranded by a promise that never settles permanently shrinks available() and every tick reports slots-exhausted. investigationsInFlight (scheduler.cjs:2920) is a bare ++/-- with MAX_CONCURRENT_INVESTIGATIONS = 1 (:2921) and no reconcile site, so exactly ONE leaked increment parks the whole fix-chain ladder until restart. quietMachineLease.heldBySlug is process-local with no reconciliation.
- mechanism (as synthesized): New `src/main/lib/schedulerRuntimeState.cjs` exporting pure `reconcileRuntimeCounters({rows, pidAlive, slots, lease, worktree, now})` → {repairs:[{counter, from, to}]}, called by dispatchLoop on every pass (and once at boot before the first dispatch). It rebuilds the derived tier from the durable tier: (a) sessionSlots.acquire gains an owner pid and a new `reconcile(liveOwners)` drops holder tokens whose owner slug is not a running row or whose pid is dead — mirroring reserveWorktreeSlot's observed-vs-counter self-heal; (b) quietMachineLease gains reconcile(liveSlugs) and is released when its holder slug is not running; (c) investigationsInFlight stops being a ++/-- counter and becomes a derived value recomputed from rows with status 'investigating' and a live pid; (d) gitWorktree's observed-count resync runs unconditionally each pass instead of only at cap, so an UNDER-count is corrected too. Every repair emits appendAuditEvent('runtime_counter_repaired', {counter, from, to}) so leak frequency becomes measurable instead of invisible. Accounting only — this code never signals a process.
- files: src/main/lib/schedulerRuntimeState.cjs, src/main/lib/sessionSlots.cjs, src/main/lib/quietMachineLease.cjs, src/main/lib/gitWorktree.cjs, src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/lib/__tests__/schedulerRuntimeState.test.cjs, src/main/lib/__tests__/sessionSlots.test.cjs, vitest.config.ts
- deletes/simplifies: Deletes the investigationsInFlight ++/-- counter entirely (it becomes derived) and generalises the single existing self-heal to all four counters, so the class stops needing a new per-counter fix each time one leaks.
- memories retired: 'worktree-cap in-memory counter leak (5 leaks = machine-wide dispatch death until restart)' — the specific case was closed by 2cc28e1; this retires the class by

- DESIGN verdict: REFUTED as written — The PROBLEM is real and confirmed on disk (three sibling counters have no reconcile site), but the MECHANISM as written is unsound: three of its four sub-mechanisms convert a *synchronous reservation* into an *asynchronous observation*, and every one of them fails open — i.e. the repair itself authorizes an extra `claude -p` launch. "Accounting only — this code never signals a process" is false in effect: dropping a sessionSlots token, releasing the quiet lease, or zeroing investigationsInFlight is an admission decision, and each over-admission path is precisely the >3-concurrent-Opus OOM class these modules were written to prevent. ### watchdog-dispatch-liveness — Teach the external watchdog to see a logically-dead-but-cosmetically-alive scheduler

- classes: dispatch liveness, observability, executor process lifecycle; estimate 25 min; depends on: heartbeat-build-health, productive-tick-clock
- problem: scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs supervises exactly one thing: heartbeat freshness (readLastHeartbeatTs + DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS = 180_000 in src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs:17) then maybeRelaunchApp. The heartbeat payload carries no dispatch-liveness field at all, and the heartbeat writer is the same 60 s setInterval that runs runQueueStarvationWatchdog — so the only condition the external watchdog can detect is one the in-process watchdog would have caught anyway. A process whose tick chain is wedged, whose loop never re-armed, or whose config leaves dispatch permanently disarmed reports 'alive / heartbeat-fresh' forever and is never relaunched. checkAppLiveness (watchdogHelpers.cjs:152) has the mirror hole: a stale heartbeat whose recorded pid has been recycled returns alive:'pid-alive-heartbeat-stale'.
- mechanism (as synthesized): (1) appendHeartbeat's payload gains `dispatch: {lastDispatchAttemptAt, lastProductiveTickAt, lastTickReason, pendingDispatchable, runningCount, paused, drain, tickWedged}`. (2) watchdogHelpers.cjs gains `DISPATCH_DEAD_MS = 30 * 60_000` and pure `evaluateDispatchLiveness(entry, now, {deadMs})` → {relaunch, reason}: relaunch only when the heartbeat is FRESH and lastProductiveTickAt is older than deadMs and pendingDispatchable > 0 and runningCount === 0 and not paused and no drain in progress — deliberately conjunctive so it can never fire while a job is in flight. (3) scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs gains a third decision branch calling maybeRelaunchApp with reason 'dispatch-dead-heartbeat-fresh', reusing the EXISTING debounce and cap state (readRelaunchState/writeRelaunchState, DEFAULT_RELAUNCH_DEBOUNCE_MS = 90_000, DEFAULT_MAX_RELAUNCH_ATTEMPTS = 3) so it cannot relaunch-loop. The watchdog stays read-only with respect to queue state, per its own header ban.
- files: src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs, scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs, src/main/lib/__tests__/watchdogHelpers.test.cjs
- deletes/simplifies: Makes the one process that runs automatically (systemd, every 2 min, from the repo checkout — so it is immune to the npx build lag) able to detect the failure the in-process machinery cannot report about itself, without adding a second relaunch mechanism.
- memories retired: 'dispatch has no driver … lastRunAt disarms watchdog' — final residue: the external watchdog can now distinguish 'process alive' from 'dispatch alive'
- PREMISE verdict: stands — The premise reproduces exactly at HEAD. `scripts/scheduler-watchdog.cjs` `main()` reads ONE signal (`readLastHeartbeatTs` vs `DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS`), computes `const decision = fresh ? 'alive' : 'stale'`, and on `fresh` does nothing but `runHistoryFinalize()` + `process.exit(0)`. No dispatch-liveness symbol exists anywhere in the repo: a repo-wide grep for `evaluateDispatchLiveness`, `DISPATCH_DEAD_MS`, `dispatch-dead-heartbeat-fresh`, `SM_WATCHDOG_DISPATCH_RELAUNCH_DISABLE` returns zero hits, and `lastProductiveTickAt`, `lastTickReason`, `tickWedged`, `pendingDispatchable` return zero hits (only `lastDispatchAttemptAt` exists, and only in queue state, never in the heartbeat). `appendHeartbeat`'s only call site emits `{ts, pid, counts, stall, paused, nextReset, utilization, consecutiveFailures}` — no dispatch block, confirming the external watchdog is structurally blind to dispatch death. 
  - suggested: Keep the PRD (premise confirmed) but amend three things before queuing. (1) Fix the file paths: tests belong in the EXISTING /home/bilko/Projects/session-manager/src/main/lib/__tests__/watchdog-relaunch.test.cjs (relaunch-decision coverage) and watchdog-helpers.test.cjs (pure helpers) — drop the invented watchdogHelpers.test.cjs; also drop the scripts/lib caveat, commit d1edf15 already completed that move. (2) Specify the maybeRelaunchApp change explicitly: add an opt-in `forceReason`/`skipLivenessGate` option so the dispatch branch can bypass the `if (liveness.alive) return {reason:'alive'}` early return, and give the dispatch branch its OWN attempt counter key inside the shared state file (e.g. `{dispatchAttemptCount, dispatchLastAttemptTs}`) so the heartbeat-fresh alive-reset cannot zero it — otherwise the cap acceptance criterion is untestable and the branch is dead code. (3) Resolve
- DESIGN verdict: REFUTED as written — The DETECTION half of this PRD (steps 1 and 2 — dispatch fields in the heartbeat payload plus a pure `evaluateDispatchLiveness`) is sound, cheap, and fills a real hole. The ACTION half (step 3 — "a third decision branch calling maybeRelaunchApp with reason 'dispatch-dead-heartbeat-fresh', reusing the EXISTING debounce and cap state") is unsound as written, for three independently verifiable reasons. (A) It is a no-op by construction. `maybeRelaunchApp` (src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs:238) takes no `reason` parameter and its FIRST action is `const liveness = checkAppLiveness({heartbeatPath, maxAgeMs})`. The new branch fires only when the heartbeat is FRESH, so `checkAppLiveness` returns `{alive:true, reason:'heartbeat-fresh'}` and `maybeRelaunchApp` returns `{relaunched:false, reason:'alive'}` without ever calling `spawnFn`. The PRD names no bypass/force parameter, so as specified the b

### proc-identity-lock — Process identity (pid + start-ticks + cmdline) so a recycled pid can never pin or kill the wrong thing

- classes: executor process lifecycle vs the app process, dispatch liveness; estimate 25 min; depends on: test-sandbox-guard
- problem: src/main/lib/instanceLock.cjs's holder check is a bare `pidAlive()` (process.kill(pid,0), EPERM counted alive) over a lock body of only {pid, startedAt}. After a hard crash the recorded pid can be recycled by any unrelated process and acquireSchedulerOwnership then refuses ownership forever — the app boots, the UI works, the scheduler never dispatches, and because a passive instance writes no heartbeat the external watchdog relaunches repeatedly until DEFAULT_MAX_RELAUNCH_ATTEMPTS and gives up. releaseSchedulerOwnership is wired only to before-quit (src/main/index.cjs:1484) and not to runShutdownCleanup (:1450), which the app.exit() paths call. checkAppLiveness (watchdogHelpers.cjs:152) has the same hole, and killOrphanClaudePid (scheduler.cjs:2200) guards only on /\bclaude\b/ against the cmdline — which matches an interactive human `claude` session holding a recycled pid.
- mechanism (as synthesized): New `src/main/lib/procIdentity.cjs` (plain Node, no electron — the watchdog loads it too): `readProcStartTicks(pid)` (/proc/<pid>/stat field 22), `readCmdline(pid)`, `identity(pid)` → {pid, startTicks, cmdline} | null, `sameProcess(a, b)`. instanceLock.writeLockExclusive writes {pid, startedAt, startTicks, cmdline} and acquireSchedulerOwnership treats a live pid whose identity differs as STALE and breaks the lock. index.cjs moves `acquireSchedulerOwnership()` to the top of the whenReady body (before createWindow and the attachWindow calls) so a passive instance never builds IPC/watchers first, and calls releaseSchedulerOwnership() from runShutdownCleanup so app.exit() paths release it. watchdogHelpers.checkAppLiveness's 'pid-alive-heartbeat-stale' branch requires sameProcess against the identity recorded in the heartbeat. scheduler.killOrphanClaudePid requires an identity match rather than a cmdline regex.
- files: src/main/lib/procIdentity.cjs, src/main/lib/instanceLock.cjs, src/main/index.cjs, src/main/lib/watchdogHelpers.cjs, src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/lib/__tests__/procIdentity.test.cjs, src/main/lib/__tests__/instanceLock.test.cjs
- deletes/simplifies: Deletes the /\bclaude\b/ cmdline heuristic in killOrphanClaudePid and the pid-only staleness test in two places, replacing three ad-hoc liveness guesses with one identity primitive.

### job-supervisor-record — Write a durable per-run supervisor record at spawn, synchronously

- classes: executor process lifecycle vs the app process, state durability; estimate 25 min; depends on: proc-identity-lock, sched-paths-resolver
- problem: The only durable record of a dispatch is `runtime = {pid, runId, startedAt, sessionId, cwd}`, written ASYNCHRONOUSLY after the spawn by the onPid callback's mutate — and it demonstrably fails to land: two rows are `running` with runtime.pid undefined right now while their executors are live and their run logs contain `[scheduler] spawned pid=`. The run dir gets only `<slug>.log`; `<slug>.meta.json` is written exclusively from exit paths. So a booting process has nothing to distinguish 'my run, still healthy' from 'abandoned orphan' — it either kills a live executor or re-queues a second `claude -p` onto the same PRD in the same tree.
- mechanism (as synthesized): New `src/main/lib/jobSupervisorRecord.cjs`: `writeSupervisorRecord({runDir, slug, cwd, runId, pid, pgid, execCwd, worktreeDir, worktreeBranch, sessionId, startedAt, budgetMs, maxDurationMs, idleKillMs, schedulerPid, codeSha})` writes `<runDir>/<slug>.supervisor.json` SYNCHRONOUSLY (config.cjs writeJsonSync — this runs in the main process) from inside withChildAndLog's post-spawn block, at the same synchronous point that already emits `[scheduler] spawned pid=`, so it cannot fail the way the async mutate does. It records pgid via process.getpgid(child.pid) and the procIdentity triple (startTicks + cmdline) so PID recycling is provably defeated. Readers: `readSupervisorRecord(runDir, slug)`, `listLiveSupervisorRecords(runsDir, {maxAgeMs})` and a PURE `classifyAdoption(record, {identity, pidAlive, logMtimeMs, now})` → 'adopt' | 'dead' | 'foreign-pid' | 'over-budget'. worktreeBranch is branchNameFor('job', slug) and worktreeDir is worktreeDirFor('job', cwd, slug) (src/main/lib/gitWorktree.cjs:294/298). src/main/lib/runLogRetention.cjs sweeps `<slug>.supervisor.json` alongside the log so the run dir cannot leak a second file per dispatch.
- files: src/main/lib/jobSupervisorRecord.cjs, src/main/lib/childWithLog.cjs, src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/lib/runLogRetention.cjs, src/main/lib/__tests__/jobSupervisorRecord.test.cjs, src/main/__tests__/scheduler-supervisor-record.test.cjs, vitest.config.ts
- deletes/simplifies: Replaces three separate liveness inferences (runtime.pid, the log's `spawned pid=` line, a /proc cwd scan) with one authoritative record; the inference ladder stays only as the fallback for rows dispatched before this landed.
- memories retired: none

### boot-adopt-executors — Boot adopts live executors instead of SIGTERMing them

- classes: executor process lifecycle vs the app process, state durability; estimate 30 min; depends on: job-supervisor-record, runtime-counter-reconcile
- problem: Boot reconciliation unconditionally kills every executor it just proved ALIVE: after `partitionBootOrphans(bootSnap.jobs)` (src/main/scheduler.cjs:3384), the deferred branch calls killOrphanClaudePid(pid) → process.kill(-pid,'SIGTERM') + SIGKILL — while three blocks earlier buildJobWorktreeIsLive deliberately SPARES that same run's checkout. Worse, partitionBootOrphans' liveness test is strictly weaker than the live reaper's: it reads only `j.runtime?.pid` + claudePidAlive, with none of readSpawnedPidFromLog / findLiveProcessForJob / isLogFresh that selectReapableJobs gained in 4b60a04/85dcaa3/8bf2d83 — so a running row whose pid mutate never landed is classified 'immediate', read as no_result and RE-QUEUED, producing a second `claude -p` on the same PRD in the same tree as the live first one. Both shapes are live in the queue right now.
- mechanism (as synthesized): Replace the `for (const slug of deferredSlugs) { killOrphanClaudePid(pid) }` loop with `adoptLiveExecutors()`. partitionBootOrphans gains the same dependency set selectReapableJobs already takes — {pidAlive: claudePidAlive, getLogPid: readSpawnedPidFromLog, findLiveProcess: findLiveProcessForJob, getLogMtimeMs: readLogMtimeMs, logFreshWindowMs: IDLE_OUTPUT_KILL_MS, readRecord: readSupervisorRecord} — so its liveness test stops being weaker than the reaper's. For each running row, resolve liveness from the supervisor record FIRST (classifyAdoption), falling back to that ladder. 'adopt' keeps status running, re-stamps runtime.pid/pgid, re-acquires a sessionSlots token for the adopted owner (so the slot pool remains the only concurrency cap and adopted runs are counted), and re-arms the wall-clock budget from the record's own budgetMs/maxDurationMs/idleKillMs via superviseAdoptedRuns() on the dispatch loop. Only 'dead' and 'foreign-pid' reach applyOrphanOutcome. Adoption never falls back to findLiveProcessForJob's bare runCwd branch (four executors currently share one cwd). The adopted run's EXIT is already owned by reapDeadRunningJobs, which terminalizes a vanished running row with salvage and landedCommit evidence — no second finalize path is introduced. Counters are re-seeded by reconcileRuntimeCounters before the first dispatch.
- files: src/main/scheduler.cjs, src/main/lib/reaperHelpers.cjs, src/main/lib/jobSupervisorRecord.cjs, src/main/__tests__/scheduler-boot-orphans.test.cjs
- deletes/simplifies: Deletes the boot kill loop outright and deletes the asymmetry whereby the boot path had none of the four liveness signals the steady-state reaper gained over four separate fix commits.

Every slug in `deferredSlugs` is by construction a row `partitionBootOrphans` just PROVED alive (`if (pid && isAlive(pid)) deferred.push(j.slug)`), so boot kills precisely the executors it proved running. (2) The asymmetry with the worktree sweep is real and sits ~20 lines earlier in the same `init()`: `buildJobWorktreeIsLive` (src/main/lib/jobWorktreeBootLive.cjs) spares a checkout when "its owning queue row is still status:'running' with a runtime.pid that claudePidAlive reports alive" — the same predicate the kill loop then uses to select victim
